A list of listeners without `std::function` (Phase 15, Day 195): a function pointer and a user pointer each, disconnected by token.

The canvas widgets draw on (Phase 15, Day 189): an abstract 2D drawing surface in logical units.

A widget never builds vertices: it calls the operations of [`ui::Canvas`](#ui-canvas), and the implementation decides what that means. The implementation of the toolkit is [`ui::DrawListCanvas`](#ui-drawlistcanvas) (it appends to a [`ui::DrawList`](#ui-drawlist) that the renderer draws); [`ui::RecordingCanvas`](#ui-recordingcanvas) records the calls for tests. A vector canvas, a software rasteriser or an exporter can be added without touching a widget. No draw-list, GPU or RHI type appears in this header.

**Units and snapping (the contract of every implementation).** Widgets work in logical units; `Scale()` physical pixels make one unit (1, 1.25, 2, ...).

- A rectangle is snapped to whole pixels by rounding its **edges**: `left = round(x * scale)`, `right = round((x + w) * scale)`, so two rectangles that share an edge in logical units share it in pixels, with no gap and no overlap. A non-empty rectangle is never thinner than one pixel.
- A border, a stroke width or any other thickness is scaled and then rounded to whole pixels, never below one pixel while it is above zero: 1 unit is 1 pixel at scale 1, 2 pixels at scale 2, and 1 pixel at scale 1.25 (never 1.25).
- Corner radii and blur distances are scaled but not rounded; a radius larger than half of the shorter side is reduced to it.
- Lines and triangles have arbitrary angles: their points are scaled, not snapped.
- Text is laid out by the caller **in physical pixels** (`text::TextStyle::pixel_size` is the logical size times `Scale()`); its origin is logical and is scaled, and the glyphs then land on whole pixels as `renderer::TextRenderer` places them.
- A colour is packed RGBA with red in the lowest byte; a fully transparent colour draws nothing.

The clipboard as the toolkit sees it (Phase 15, Day 197): set text, ask for text and be called back. The in-process implementation serves offscreen surfaces and tests; the OS-backed one (`ui::OsClipboard` in `ui_window`: asynchronous, with the primary selection) sits behind the same interface (Day 200), so a widget written against this does not change.

The debug overlay (Phase 15, Day 203): a frame-time graph, counters fed by the program, and a log console with a command line.

Drag and drop in the toolkit (Phase 15, Day 200): what is dragged. The routing is in [`ui::Surface`](#ui-surface); the widgets that start and accept drags override the `Widget` drag virtuals.

The 2D draw list (Phase 15, Day 189): the data [`ui::DrawListCanvas`](#ui-drawlistcanvas) builds and the renderer draws. Vertices, 32-bit indices and commands; no GPU type.

Positions are physical pixels with the origin at the top-left of the surface. Every primitive is a quad (4 vertices, 6 indices) except a triangle (3, 3); the fragment shader picks its work from the **mode** in the low bits of `Vertex::flags`, so rectangles, glyphs and images of one frame share one pipeline and a command is split only by clip, atlas page or image.

The canvas that builds a [`ui::DrawList`](#ui-drawlist) (Phase 15, Day 189).

The events a surface delivers to its widgets (Phase 15, Day 192): pointer, wheel, key, text, focus and tick, as plain data.

The plain geometry types of the UI toolkit: points, rectangles and corner radii in logical units, and the whole-pixel rectangle the draw list clips with.

The container widgets (Phase 15, Day 193): [`ui::BoxLayout`](#ui-boxlayout), [`ui::StackLayout`](#ui-stacklayout), [`ui::AnchorLayout`](#ui-anchorlayout), [`ui::GridLayout`](#ui-gridlayout) and [`ui::Spacer`](#ui-spacer).

Layout is two steps per widget. `Measure(constraints)` says what size the widget wants from its content; `Arrange(rect)` places it and its children. Everything is in logical units and fractional; the canvas snaps rectangles to whole pixels when drawing, so neighbours share an edge at any scale. A child that is collapsed takes no space; one that is only hidden keeps it. Children read their `LayoutProps` (weight, alignment, minimum and maximum size, grid cell, anchors).

The vocabulary of layout (Phase 15, Day 193): constraints, alignment and the per-widget layout properties the containers read.

The seam between a surface and the window's pointer lock (Phase 15, Day 201): `ui` names no window, so the window adapter implements this.

Popups and accelerators, the plain types (Phase 15, Day 198): where a popup goes relative to its anchor, how it closes, and the key chords of accelerators.

A settings screen generated from the registry (Phase 15, Day 203): one labelled row per setting, the widget chosen by its type, changes written through `Settings::Apply`.

The surface (Phase 15, Day 192): the root of a widget tree with its size, scale, clock, theme, font provider, focus, pointer capture and popup layer. It knows no window.

Input for a surface that has no window (Phase 15, Day 202): a screen in the world that the application feeds from a ray cast, a replay or a test.

The offscreen test harness (Phase 15, Day 192): a surface with no window, a font, an atlas and helpers to send input, run frames and read rectangles and pixels.

A canvas that records the calls instead of drawing them, so a widget test can say "this button drew a rounded rect with the hover colour" (Phase 15, Day 189).

A small CPU rasteriser for a [`ui::DrawList`](#ui-drawlist) (Phase 15, Day 192), so widget tests compare pixels without a GPU. For tests only; the renderer is the real thing.

It follows the UI shader (`shaders/ui.frag.slang`): solid, rounded-rectangle (anti-aliased, with ring and shadow) and glyph modes, premultiplied blending, the scissor of each command, a target that is not sRGB (colours are written as given). Image-mode commands are skipped (a software test has no image textures).

A block of text that sizes itself from its content (Phase 15, Day 193): the base of the label widget.

A string laid out in one font, cached (Phase 15, Day 195): what a label, a button and a check box draw their text from.

Word boundaries for editing widgets (Phase 15, Day 197): a word is a run of letters, digits and underscore; a run of punctuation is a word of its own; white space separates.

The classes come from code point ranges, not a full Unicode table (the foundation has none): ASCII, the Latin-1 and general punctuation blocks, the CJK and full-width punctuation and the usual spaces are told apart; every other code point counts as a letter. Offsets are byte offsets into UTF-8 text and fall on code point starts; a caller that must stay on grapheme boundaries snaps them (the text field does, through `text/cursor.h`).

The look of the toolkit as data: colors, metrics, fonts and per-widget styles that every widget draws from (Phase 15, Day 191).

A [`ui::Theme`](#ui-theme) is plain data. A widget asks a [`ui::ThemeSet`](#ui-themeset) for its look by kind, variant and state; the set walks a chain (the built-in default, the surface's theme, the overrides of the widget and its ancestors), a value left unset falls to the next level, and the result is scaled to physical pixels. Colors are `0xAABBGGRR` (the draw list's format); all metrics and font sizes are logical units.

When a surface runs a frame (Phase 15, Day 202): every frame, only when something changed, or at a fixed rate. A screen in the world that nobody touches should cost nothing.

The viewport widget (Phase 15, Day 201): a rectangle that shows a rendered 3D world and hands the input a camera needs to the application. No RHI type appears here: the application implements [`ui::ViewportBinding`](#ui-viewportbinding) over its own render target (the renderer's `ViewportTarget` is one).

The retained widget tree (Phase 15, Day 192): a [`ui::Widget`](#ui-widget) owns its children, belongs to one [`ui::Surface`](#ui-surface) and draws through the abstract [`ui::Canvas`](#ui-canvas).

The button widgets (Phase 15, Day 195): button, toggle button, check box, radio button and radio group.

The combo box (Phase 15, Day 198): a closed box showing one item of a list, and a drop-down list to choose another.

The dialog (Phase 15, Day 199): a window frame with a row of buttons, a default button and a result, shown modally by `Surface::ShowModal`.

The display widgets (Phase 15, Day 195): image, progress bar, separator and panel. Labels are in `ui/widgets/label.h`, buttons in `ui/widgets/buttons.h`.

The label widget (Phase 15, Day 195).

The list box (Phase 15, Day 196): a virtualized list of text rows with single or multiple selection.

Shared drawing of the widgets of Phase 15 Day 195: a resolved style turned into a box, in logical units.

Menus (Phase 15, Day 198): the `Menu` model of items, and `MenuView`, the widget a surface shows as a popup for it.

The menu bar (Phase 15, Day 198): a row of titles, each opening a [`ui::Menu`](#ui-menu) below it.

Message boxes (Phase 15, Day 199): common dialogs built from the widgets, shown modally.

The shade under a modal dialog (Phase 15, Day 199): it covers the surface, takes every pointer event, and is drawn in the `modaldim` style.

The value range the value widgets share (Phase 15, Day 196): clamp, snap to a step exactly, and the clamp-or-reject choice.

The scrolling widgets (Phase 15, Day 196): the scroll bar and the scroll view that the list box and the text field share.

The splitter (Phase 15, Day 199): panes side by side or one over the other, with handles the user drags.

The tab view (Phase 15, Day 199): a row of tab headers over a stack of pages, of which one shows.

The single-line text field (Phase 15, Day 197): UTF-8 text edited by grapheme cluster with the keyboard and the mouse, a selection, word movement, undo and redo, and the clipboard.

The tooltip bubble (Phase 15, Day 198): a short text in a box near the pointer, shown by the surface for a widget with a tooltip text.

The widgets that hold a number (Phase 15, Day 196): the slider and the spin box.

The window frame (Phase 15, Day 199): a panel with a title bar, a content area and an optional footer, that the user can move and resize inside its parent.

## `Callback` {#ui-callback}

`class ui::Callback` — defined in `engine/ui/callback.h`

```cpp
template <typename... Args>
class Callback { ... }
```

A set of listeners that are called with `Args...`.

A listener is a plain function `void (*)(void* user, Args...)` and the `user` pointer given when it was connected. Listeners are called in the order they were connected. A listener may connect or disconnect (itself or another) while the callback runs: the call goes on over a snapshot, and a listener disconnected during the call is not called.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the list; the user pointers are borrowed and must outlive the connection. |

## `Canvas` {#ui-canvas}

`class ui::Canvas` — defined in `engine/ui/canvas.h`

```cpp
class Canvas { ... }
```

The 2D drawing surface widgets draw on.

Drawing order is call order inside a layer; layers are drawn in the order of their numbers (see [`ui::Canvas::BeginLayer`](#ui-canvas-beginlayer-i32)). Clips (see [`ui::Canvas::PushClip`](#ui-canvas-pushclip-const-rect-amp)) apply to everything drawn while they are on the stack.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | not-thread-safe; owner-thread only. |
| Ownership | owns nothing by itself; implementations document what they borrow. Not copyable. |

## `Clipboard` {#ui-clipboard}

`class ui::Clipboard` — defined in `engine/ui/clipboard.h`

```cpp
class Clipboard { ... }
```

Where copied text goes and pasted text comes from.

Pasting is asynchronous in shape: `RequestPaste` takes a function that is called with the text, either before `RequestPaste` returns (the in-process clipboard) or later, from the surface's thread (an OS clipboard that has to ask another program). A caller therefore must not assume either, and cancels a request it no longer wants (a widget being destroyed).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the text it holds; the callback's user pointer is borrowed until the call or `CancelPaste`. |


## `LogCapture` {#ui-logcapture}

`class ui::LogCapture` — defined in `engine/ui/debug_overlay.h`

```cpp
class LogCapture { ... }
```

The log lines the console shows: a bounded copy of the log core's ring, read on the UI thread.

`Poll` reads the lines logged since the last call with `ReadLogLinesSince` (thread-safe, so any thread may log) and keeps the newest `kMaxLines`; the console itself adds its own output with `Append`. A filter keeps only the lines that contain a text (a level name such as `WARN` works, as the lines carry it).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only; the log it reads is thread-safe. |
| Ownership | owns the copied lines. |

### `kMaxLines` {#ui-logcapture-kmaxlines}

`variable ui::LogCapture::kMaxLines` — defined in `engine/ui/debug_overlay.h`

```cpp
static constexpr usize kMaxLines;
```

The most lines kept; older ones are dropped.

| Returns | how many lines were added. |

## `DebugOverlay` {#ui-debugoverlay}

`class ui::DebugOverlay` — defined in `engine/ui/debug_overlay.h`

```cpp
class DebugOverlay : public BoxLayout { ... }
```

The overlay: the graph, a line of counters, the log console and a command line, in a panel at the top right of the surface.

`Attach` adds it to a surface and registers the key that shows and hides it (hidden it is collapsed: it costs no layout, no draw and no `Update`). While it is shown it polls the log each frame, so the console follows what any thread logs. A line typed in the command line runs when Enter is pressed: its first word names a command registered with `AddCommand`, the rest is the argument. `help` lists the commands, `clear` empties the console. The command's text output goes to the console through the `LogCapture` the function receives.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its widgets and the capture; borrows the commands' user pointers. |

### `CommandFn` {#ui-debugoverlay-commandfn}

`alias ui::DebugOverlay::CommandFn` — defined in `engine/ui/debug_overlay.h`

```cpp

```

What a command does: it gets the pointer given to `AddCommand`, the text after the command's name (trimmed), and the capture to write lines of output to.

The width of the panel in logical units.

The gap between the panel and the surface edge.

The most commands that can be registered.

## `DragPayload` {#ui-dragpayload}

`class ui::DragPayload` — defined in `engine/ui/drag_drop.h`

```cpp
class DragPayload { ... }
```

What a drag carries: typed entries, the actions the source allows, and the action the user asks for.

A source builds one with the `Add` functions and gives it to [`ui::Surface::BeginDrag`](#ui-surface-begindrag-dragpayload-uniqueptr-lt-widget-gt-point). A target reads it in `CanAccept` and `OnDrop`; a payload that came from another program is `pending` until the drop (its entries name the kinds on offer without data), so a target must not read bytes in `CanAccept`. Every entry is capped at [`ui::DragPayload::kMaxEntryBytes`](#ui-dragpayload-kmaxentrybytes) and text is made valid UTF-8, so a hostile source cannot hand a target more than a clipboard could.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | owns its entries. |

### `kMaxEntries` {#ui-dragpayload-kmaxentries}

`variable ui::DragPayload::kMaxEntries` — defined in `engine/ui/drag_drop.h`

```cpp
static constexpr usize kMaxEntries;
```

The most entries one payload holds.

### `kMaxEntryBytes` {#ui-dragpayload-kmaxentrybytes}

`variable ui::DragPayload::kMaxEntryBytes` — defined in `engine/ui/drag_drop.h`

```cpp
static constexpr usize kMaxEntryBytes;
```

The most bytes one entry holds.

`method ui::DragPayload::AddText` — defined in `engine/ui/drag_drop.h`

```cpp
bool AddText(StringView text);
```

Adds a text entry (invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|

The vertices, indices and commands of one frame of UI.

`variable ui::DrawList::max_vertices` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list.h`

```cpp

```

The most vertices the list holds; a draw past it is dropped and counted in `stats.overflowed`. The default keeps both the vertex numbers and the index count (1.5 per quad vertex) within 32 bits.

|---|---|

`method ui::DrawList::SortLayers` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list.h`

```cpp
void SortLayers();
```

Orders the commands by layer, keeping call order inside a layer (a stable insertion sort that allocates nothing; a frame has a handful of commands, and an already ordered list costs one pass). The index data stays where it is: a command names its own range.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

## `DrawListCanvas` {#ui-drawlistcanvas}

`class ui::DrawListCanvas` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
class DrawListCanvas final : public Canvas { ... }
```

A [`ui::Canvas`](#ui-canvas) that converts logical units to physical pixels, snaps as the canvas contract says, and appends vertices, indices and commands to a draw list.

A frame is `list.Clear()`, `Begin(scale, width, height)`, any number of draws, then `list.SortLayers()`. Clips and layers are physical-pixel rectangles on stacks that keep their capacity. A draw whose rectangle does not meet the current clip, or that is fully transparent, makes no vertex. Consecutive draws with the same clip, layer and texture share a command; a draw that needs no texture joins any command.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | not-thread-safe; owner-thread only. |
| Ownership | borrows the draw list and the glyph atlas, which must outlive it. |

### `DrawListCanvas(DrawList*,text::GlyphAtlas*)` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-drawlistcanvas-drawlist-star-text-glyphatlas-star}

`constructor ui::DrawListCanvas::DrawListCanvas` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
DrawListCanvas(DrawList* list, text::GlyphAtlas* atlas);
```

Makes a canvas that appends to `list` and takes glyphs from `atlas`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `list` | the draw list to build; borrowed. |
| Parameter `atlas` | the glyph atlas for text; borrowed; null makes every glyph count as dropped. |
| Aborts | `list` is null. |
| Ownership | the list and the atlas are borrowed. |

### `Rebind(DrawList*,text::GlyphAtlas*)` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-rebind-drawlist-star-text-glyphatlas-star}

```cpp
void Rebind(DrawList* list, text::GlyphAtlas* atlas);
```

Points the canvas at another list and atlas, keeping its stacks' storage (so a surface that draws every frame allocates nothing in steady state).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `list` | the draw list to build; borrowed. |
| Parameter `atlas` | the glyph atlas for text; borrowed; null makes every glyph count as dropped. |
| Aborts | `list` is null. |
| Ownership | the list and the atlas are borrowed. |

```cpp
void PushClip(const Rect& rect) override;
```

Restricts drawing to the intersection of the current clip and `rect`.

_Inherited from [`ui::Canvas::PushClip(const Rect&)`](#ui-canvas-pushclip-const-rect-amp)._

### `PopClip()` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-popclip}

`method ui::DrawListCanvas::PopClip` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
void PopClip() override;
```

Removes the clip pushed last.

_Inherited from [`ui::Canvas::PopClip()`](#ui-canvas-popclip)._

### `BeginLayer(i32)` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-beginlayer-i32}

`method ui::DrawListCanvas::BeginLayer` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
void BeginLayer(i32 order) override;
```

Starts a layer: what is drawn until [`ui::Canvas::EndLayer`](#ui-canvas-endlayer) comes above everything drawn in lower layers, whatever the order of the calls. Equal layers keep call order. A layer starts with no clip (the whole surface), so a popup is not cut by the clip of the widget that opened it; the clips of the outer layer return at the end.

_Inherited from [`ui::Canvas::BeginLayer(i32)`](#ui-canvas-beginlayer-i32)._

### `EndLayer()` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-endlayer}

`method ui::DrawListCanvas::EndLayer` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
void EndLayer() override;
```

Ends the layer started last and restores the clips that were active before it.

_Inherited from [`ui::Canvas::EndLayer()`](#ui-canvas-endlayer)._

### `CurrentClip()` {#ui-drawlistcanvas-currentclip-const}

`method ui::DrawListCanvas::CurrentClip` — defined in `engine/ui/draw_list_canvas.h`

```cpp
PixelRect CurrentClip() const;
```

Returns the current clip in physical pixels.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

```cpp
using Key = os::Key;
```

## `EventType` {#ui-eventtype}

`enum ui::EventType` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
enum class EventType : u8 { ... }
```

The kind of a [`ui::Event`](#ui-event).

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="ui-eventtype-none"></a>`None` |  | No event. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-pointermove"></a>`PointerMove` |  | The pointer moved to `position`. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-pointerdown"></a>`PointerDown` |  | A button went down at `position` (`button` is an [`os::MouseButtonBit`](platform-window-input.md#os-mousebuttonbit)). |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-pointerup"></a>`PointerUp` |  | A button went up at `position`. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-pointerenter"></a>`PointerEnter` |  | The pointer entered the widget; sent by the surface, never bubbles. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-pointerleave"></a>`PointerLeave` |  | The pointer left the widget; sent by the surface, never bubbles. Posted to the surface it means the pointer left the surface: the hover is cleared. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-wheel"></a>`Wheel` |  | The wheel turned by (`wheel_x`, `wheel_y`) notches at `position`. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-keydown"></a>`KeyDown` |  | A key went down (or repeated); goes to the focus owner. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-keyup"></a>`KeyUp` |  | A key went up; goes to the focus owner. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-text"></a>`Text` |  | One Unicode scalar typed, as UTF-8 in `text`; goes to the focus owner. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-focusgained"></a>`FocusGained` |  | The widget got the keyboard focus; sent by the surface, never bubbles. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-focuslost"></a>`FocusLost` |  | The widget lost the keyboard focus; sent by the surface, never bubbles. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-windowfocusgained"></a>`WindowFocusGained` |  | Posted to the surface: the window it shows in got the focus (the focus owner gets `FocusGained` again). |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-windowfocuslost"></a>`WindowFocusLost` |  | Posted to the surface: the window lost the focus; capture is released, popups close, the modifier mirror resets and the focus owner gets `FocusLost`. |
| <a id="ui-eventtype-relativemotion"></a>`RelativeMotion` |  | While the pointer is locked: it moved by (`rel_x`, `rel_y`) device units; goes to the widget that asked for the lock (Day 201), never bubbles. |

## `Event` {#ui-event}

`struct ui::Event` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
struct Event { ... }
```

One event.

Posted to a surface with `position` in surface coordinates (logical units); a widget receives it with `position` in its own local space (its top-left corner is the origin). A widget that handles an event sets `handled`; otherwise it bubbles to the parent, up to the root. Enter, leave and focus events go to one widget only.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

### `type` {#ui-event-type}

`variable ui::Event::type` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
EventType type;
```

What happened.

### `handled` {#ui-event-handled}

`variable ui::Event::handled` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
bool handled;
```

Set by a widget that dealt with the event; stops bubbling.

### `repeat` {#ui-event-repeat}

`variable ui::Event::repeat` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
bool repeat;
```

`KeyDown`: an auto-repeat.

### `text_length` {#ui-event-text-length}

`variable ui::Event::text_length` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
u8 text_length;
```

`Text`: bytes used in `text`.

### `key` {#ui-event-key}

`variable ui::Event::key` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
Key key;
```

`KeyDown`, `KeyUp`: the key.

### `text` {#ui-event-text}

`variable ui::Event::text` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
char text[4];
```

`Text`: the scalar as UTF-8.

### `modifiers` {#ui-event-modifiers}

`variable ui::Event::modifiers` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
u32 modifiers;
```

Mask of [`os::ModifierBit`](platform-window-input.md#os-modifierbit) values.

### `button` {#ui-event-button}

`variable ui::Event::button` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
u32 button;
```

`PointerDown`, `PointerUp`: an [`os::MouseButtonBit`](platform-window-input.md#os-mousebuttonbit) value.

### `position` {#ui-event-position}

`variable ui::Event::position` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp
Point position;
```

Pointer events: where, in logical units (surface or local space, see above).

### `wheel_x` {#ui-event-wheel-x}

`variable ui::Event::wheel_x` — defined in `engine/ui/event.h`

```cpp

```

`Wheel`: horizontal notches, positive to the right.

## `BoxLayout` {#ui-boxlayout}

`class ui::BoxLayout` — defined in `engine/ui/layout.h`

```cpp
class BoxLayout : public Widget { ... }
```

A container that gives every shown child a cell along one axis: preferred sizes first, then free space to the children with a weight (up to their maximum), or, when there is too little room, each child shrinks towards its minimum in proportion to how far above it is. Across the axis a child follows its alignment.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its children. |

## `LayoutProps` {#ui-layoutprops}

`struct ui::LayoutProps` — defined in `engine/ui/layout_types.h`

```cpp
struct LayoutProps { ... }
```

What a widget tells its container about itself.

A width or height of -1 means "from the content" (`Widget::Measure`); any other value is the preferred size, which a container uses to size the widget when it is not stretched (`Align::Stretch` fills the cell instead, so set `min` and `max` equal, or an alignment other than `Stretch`, for a fixed size). The size is always limited to `[min, max]`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

Preferred width, or -1 for the content's.

## `PlacePopup(Size,const Rect&,Placement,const Rect&)` {#ui-placepopup-size-const-rect-amp-placement-const-rect-amp}

`function ui::PlacePopup` — defined in `engine/ui/popup.h`

```cpp
Rect PlacePopup(Size size, const Rect& anchor, Placement placement, const Rect& bounds);
```

Returns where a popup of `size` goes for `anchor`, kept inside `bounds`.

The preferred side is used when the popup fits there; otherwise the opposite side when it fits, otherwise the preferred side. Then the popup is shifted along the anchor's edge to stay inside `bounds`, and finally clamped so that it starts inside `bounds` even when it is larger.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `size` | the popup's size. |
| Parameter `anchor` | the anchor rectangle, in surface coordinates (a context menu passes an empty rectangle at the pointer). |
| Parameter `placement` | the preferred side. |
| Parameter `bounds` | the area to stay in (the surface). |
| Returns | the popup's rectangle. |
| Fails | never. |

## `SettingsView` {#ui-settingsview}

`class ui::SettingsView` — defined in `engine/ui/settings_view.h`

```cpp
class SettingsView : public BoxLayout { ... }
```

A settings screen: groups of rows, built from the metadata of a [`settings::Registry`](foundation-settings.md#settings-registry).

Rows are made for every setting whose key starts with the prefix, in key order, grouped by `SettingDesc::group` (a setting with no group is under "General"). `kHidden` settings are skipped; `kAdvanced` ones are in rows that stay collapsed until the "Show advanced settings" box is ticked. The widget follows the type: a bool is a check box; an int or float with a range is a slider and a spin box that show the same value; an enum is a combo box over its names; text is a text field (a password field for `kSecret`). Ranges, steps and the clamp-or-reject policy come from the declaration. A `kRestartRequired` setting has a note "restart required" in its row, and `RestartPending` says one was changed.

A change goes through `Settings::Apply` (with the apply function given to `SetApply`). If it is refused, the widget goes back to the value in force and `Message` says why. A value that an environment variable, the command line or a policy layer decides is shown disabled with its source as a tooltip. The widgets show the current values, refreshed when a settings listener reports a change (from any thread) and never by polling: an idle screen reads nothing from the settings.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only; the settings listener only sets a flag, so settings may change on any thread. |
| Ownership | borrows the settings, which must outlive the view; owns its rows. |

### `kDefaultGroup` {#ui-settingsview-kdefaultgroup}


The heading of a setting that names no group.

## `Surface` {#ui-surface}

`class ui::Surface` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
class Surface { ... }
```

A tree of widgets and everything the widgets share.

A window, a test or an in-world screen feeds it events with `Post` and runs `Frame`; the surface never names a window. The frame cycle is: apply the queued events, call `Update` of the widgets that want it, run layout if dirty, and (when a draw list is given) rebuild it if anything looks different or the list is empty.

Pointer events go to the capture owner, else to the deepest widget under the pointer (popups first); a button press captures the pointer to the widget it hit until the last button is released. Key and text events go to the focus owner. An event the target does not handle bubbles to its parents. Enter and leave follow the hit widget.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the root and the popup layer; borrows the theme, the font provider and the widgets' references (focus, capture, hover), which are cleared when a widget dies. |


Makes a surface of `width` x `height` logical units at `scale`, with an empty root called "root" covering it.

### `BeginDrag(DragPayload,UniquePtr<Widget>,Point)` {#ui-surface-begindrag-dragpayload-uniqueptr-lt-widget-gt-point}

`method ui::Surface::BeginDrag` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
bool BeginDrag(DragPayload payload, UniquePtr<Widget> visual = UniquePtr<Widget>(), Point visual_offset = Point());
```

Starts a drag from the widget that holds the pointer capture (the one whose press is in progress): the surface takes the pointer, hover goes to drop targets only, the source's press ends, and `visual` (if any) follows the pointer in the top layer. The drag ends with the release of the last button (a drop on the target under the pointer, else a cancel), with Escape, or with `CancelDrag`; the source then gets `OnDragEnded`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `payload` | what is dragged; moved. Needs at least one entry and one allowed action. |
| Parameter `visual` | an optional widget tree shown at the pointer (made pass-through); its size is measured once. |
| Parameter `visual_offset` | where its top-left corner lies relative to the pointer. |
| Returns | whether the drag started. |
| Fails | returns false, changing nothing, when a drag is going on, no button is down or the payload is empty or allows nothing. |
| Ownership | the surface owns `visual` until the drag ends. |

### `OpenPopup(UniquePtr<Widget>,const Rect&,const PopupOptions&)` {#ui-surface-openpopup-uniqueptr-lt-widget-gt-const-rect-amp-const-popupoptions-amp}

`method ui::Surface::OpenPopup` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
Widget* OpenPopup(UniquePtr<Widget> widget, const Rect& anchor, const PopupOptions& options);
```

Opens `widget` in the popup layer next to `anchor` (surface coordinates), sized by its own measure and placed by [`ui::PlacePopup`](#ui-placepopup-size-const-rect-amp-placement-const-rect-amp) inside the surface.

Popups form a stack: a press outside all of them closes them all (and is consumed or passed on as `options` say), Escape that the focus owner did not handle closes the top one, and losing the window focus closes every one. Closing a popup closes the ones above it. The popup takes the keyboard focus when `options.take_focus` is set and it is focusable, and the previous owner gets it back when the last popup closes. Opening a popup hides the tooltip.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `widget` | the popup's content; the popup layer owns it until it closes. |
| Parameter `anchor` | where the popup attaches, in surface coordinates. |
| Parameter `options` | how it places, takes the focus and closes. |
| Returns | the widget, for `ClosePopup` and `IsPopupOpen`. |
| Aborts | `widget` is null. |
| Ownership | ownership passes to the surface; `options.owner` is borrowed. |

### `ShowModal(UniquePtr<Widget>)` {#ui-surface-showmodal-uniqueptr-lt-widget-gt}

`method ui::Surface::ShowModal` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
Widget* ShowModal(UniquePtr<Widget> dialog);
```

Shows `dialog` as a modal dialog: a shade (the `modaldim` style) covers everything beneath, the pointer cannot reach what lies under it, and Tab stays inside it.

The dialog is measured, centered in the surface and given the keyboard focus (its first Tab stop); the previous focus owner gets the focus back when the dialog closes. Dialogs stack: a dialog shown while another is open covers it. Keys go to the top dialog while the focus is outside it, and accelerators and the menu bar's keys do not run. Popups opened from the dialog work as usual (they are above it). Opening a dialog closes the popups and releases the pointer capture.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `dialog` | the dialog's root widget; it should be sized by its own `Measure`. |
| Returns | the dialog, for `CloseModal`. |
| Aborts | `dialog` is null. |
| Ownership | ownership passes to the surface. |

### `CloseModal(Widget*)` {#ui-surface-closemodal-widget-star}

`method ui::Surface::CloseModal` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
void CloseModal(Widget* dialog);
```

Closes `dialog` and the dialogs opened after it, removes their shades, closes the popups and gives the focus back to the owner it had when `dialog` opened (the deferral rule applies during event delivery). Does nothing for a widget that is not a modal dialog.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `dialog` | a widget `ShowModal` returned. |

### `FocusNext(bool)` {#ui-surface-focusnext-bool}

`method ui::Surface::FocusNext` — defined in `engine/ui/surface.h`

```cpp
bool FocusNext(bool backwards);
```

Moves the focus to the next (or previous) widget in Tab order and wraps at the ends; returns whether a widget took it. The order is: the widgets with a positive `TabIndex` ascending, then the ones with 0 in tree order; negative ones, disabled, hidden and collapsed widgets are skipped. While a popup is open only the popup layer is walked. With no focus, forward starts at the first and backward at the last. Tab and Shift+Tab call it when the focus owner does not handle the key.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | returns false, changing nothing, when no widget can take the focus. |

## `SurfaceQuad` {#ui-surfacequad}

`struct ui::SurfaceQuad` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
struct SurfaceQuad { ... }
```

A flat parallelogram in the world that shows a surface: `corner` is the surface's top-left corner, `edge_u` goes along its top edge to the top-right corner and `edge_v` down its left edge to the bottom-left corner.

The front face is the side from which `edge_u` points to the right and `edge_v` points down. The two edges need not be at right angles or of equal length to the surface's shape: the hit is returned as a fraction of each edge.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

### `corner` {#ui-surfacequad-corner}

`variable ui::SurfaceQuad::corner` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
f32 corner[3];
```

The top-left corner.

### `edge_u` {#ui-surfacequad-edge-u}

`variable ui::SurfaceQuad::edge_u` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
f32 edge_u[3];
```

From the top-left to the top-right corner.

### `edge_v` {#ui-surfacequad-edge-v}

`variable ui::SurfaceQuad::edge_v` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
f32 edge_v[3];
```

From the top-left to the bottom-left corner.

## `RayToSurfacePoint(const Ray3&,const SurfaceQuad&)` {#ui-raytosurfacepoint-const-ray3-amp-const-surfacequad-amp}

`function ui::RayToSurfacePoint` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
SurfaceHit RayToSurfacePoint(const Ray3& ray, const SurfaceQuad& quad);
```

Finds where a ray crosses a quad. Math only: no scene type is involved.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `ray` | the ray; a zero direction hits nothing. |
| Parameter `quad` | the quad; a degenerate one (edges parallel or of length 0) hits nothing. |
| Returns | the hit; a ray parallel to the quad, pointing away from it, or crossing outside it misses. |
| Fails | reported through the result (`hit` false), never by aborting. |

## `SurfaceInput` {#ui-surfaceinput}

`class ui::SurfaceInput` — defined in `engine/ui/surface_input.h`

```cpp
class SurfaceInput { ... }
```

Feeds a surface the events a window adapter would, from pointer positions in the surface's own pixels.

A surface with input off (the default) is display-only: every call is ignored. An interactive one reacts like any other: hover and pressed states, capture and focus work per surface. `Lost` ends what a vanished pointer leaves behind: buttons still held are released and the hover is cleared.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the surface, which must outlive this object. |

|---|---|
| Parameter `surface` | the surface; borrowed. |
| Ownership | borrows `surface`. |

## `Harness` {#ui-harness}

`class ui::Harness` — defined in `engine/ui/testing/harness.h`

```cpp
class Harness final : public FontProvider { ... }
```

Everything a widget test needs: an offscreen [`ui::Surface`](#ui-surface), a font provider that serves one font for every family key, a glyph atlas, the draw list of the last frame and the software rasteriser.

Every input helper posts one event and runs a frame with `dt` 0, so the effect is visible when it returns.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the surface, the font and the atlas. |


Makes a harness.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `width` | surface width in logical units. |
| Parameter `height` | surface height in logical units. |
| Parameter `scale` | pixels per logical unit. |
| Parameter `font_file` | path of the TrueType font served for every family; if it cannot be opened `Valid()` is false and text is dropped. |

`method ui::RecordingCanvas::BeginLayer` — defined in `engine/ui/testing/recording_canvas.h`

```cpp
void BeginLayer(i32 order) override;
```

Starts a layer: what is drawn until [`ui::Canvas::EndLayer`](#ui-canvas-endlayer) comes above everything drawn in lower layers, whatever the order of the calls. Equal layers keep call order. A layer starts with no clip (the whole surface), so a popup is not cut by the clip of the widget that opened it; the clips of the outer layer return at the end.

_Inherited from [`ui::Canvas::BeginLayer(i32)`](#ui-canvas-beginlayer-i32)._

## `TextBlock` {#ui-textblock}

`class ui::TextBlock` — defined in `engine/ui/text_block.h`

```cpp
class TextBlock : public Widget { ... }
```

Draws one string in one font and reports its size to the layout.

The text is laid out in physical pixels (font size times the surface scale) and the result converted back to logical units. With wrapping on, the width given to `Measure` is the wrap width, so the height depends on the width: a container measures the block at the width it will give it. The layout is cached on (text, font, pixel size, wrap width, font object); an unchanged block does not lay out again. The default color is the theme's `Text`, the default font the theme's `Body`; a block with a style kind (a [`ui::Label`](#ui-label)) takes both from its style in the theme instead, in the widget's state (so a disabled label uses the disabled color).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its text and the cached layout; borrows the font from the surface's font provider. |

### `TextBlock(StringView,StringView)` {#ui-textblock-textblock-stringview-stringview}

| Parameter `text` | the UTF-8 text. |

## `TextRun` {#ui-textrun}

`class ui::TextRun` — defined in `engine/ui/text_run.h`

```cpp
class TextRun { ... }
```

One string with its laid-out form, redone only when something it depends on changes.

The layout depends on the text, the alignment, the font object, the pixel size, the wrap width and the surface scale. `Fit` gives the layout that fits a width: the full one, or the text cut at a cluster boundary with an ellipsis (U+2026) appended when it is wider; the cut layout is cached too.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the text and the layouts; borrows the font only during `Ensure`. |

```cpp
bool Ensure(text::Font* face, u32 family, f32 size_px, f32 wrap_px, f32 scale);
```

Makes the full layout current for the given font and sizes.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|

| Ownership | borrows `face` for the call only. |

## `WordStart(StringView,u32)` {#ui-wordstart-stringview-u32}

`function ui::WordStart` — defined in `engine/ui/text_words.h`

```cpp
u32 WordStart(StringView text, u32 offset);
```

Returns where Ctrl+Left goes from `offset`: the start of the word before it (white space before `offset` is skipped first); 0 at the start.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `text` | UTF-8 text. |
| Parameter `offset` | a byte offset on a code point start; clamped to the text. |
| Returns | a byte offset on a code point start. |
| Fails | never. |

## `WordEnd(StringView,u32)` {#ui-wordend-stringview-u32}

`function ui::WordEnd` — defined in `engine/ui/text_words.h`

```cpp
u32 WordEnd(StringView text, u32 offset);
```

Returns where Ctrl+Right goes from `offset`: the end of the word after it (white space at `offset` is skipped first); the length at the end.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `text` | UTF-8 text. |
| Parameter `offset` | a byte offset on a code point start; clamped to the text. |
| Returns | a byte offset on a code point start. |
| Fails | never. |

## `WordRangeAt(StringView,u32,u32*,u32*)` {#ui-wordrangeat-stringview-u32-u32-star-u32-star}

`function ui::WordRangeAt` — defined in `engine/ui/text_words.h`

```cpp
void WordRangeAt(StringView text, u32 offset, u32* begin, u32* end);
```

Finds the run of characters of one class around `offset`, what a double click selects.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `text` | UTF-8 text. |
| Parameter `offset` | a byte offset; the run is the one that starts at it, or at the end of the text the last one. |
| Parameter `begin` | receives the start of the run. |
| Parameter `end` | receives the end of the run. |
| Fails | never; an empty text gives 0 and 0. |
| Ownership | writes through `begin` and `end`, which must be non-null. |

## `RegisterWidgetKind(StringView)` {#ui-registerwidgetkind-stringview}

`function ui::RegisterWidgetKind` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
WidgetKind RegisterWidgetKind(StringView name);
```

Returns the id of the widget kind called `name`, registering it on first use. Ids are stable for the life of the process and the same for the same name.

A name is never forgotten, so register a fixed set of names (literals), not text that varies.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `name` | 1 to 63 bytes. |
| Returns | the id, or 0 if the name is empty or too long. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |

## `WidgetKindName(WidgetKind)` {#ui-widgetkindname-widgetkind}


| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |

## `RegisterVariant(StringView)` {#ui-registervariant-stringview}

`function ui::RegisterVariant` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
VariantId RegisterVariant(StringView name);
```

Returns the id of the variant called `name`, registering it on first use; the empty name is variant 0, the kind's default style.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | the id, or 0 for the empty name and for a name longer than 63 bytes. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |

## `ThemeSet` {#ui-themeset}

`class ui::ThemeSet` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
class ThemeSet { ... }
```

What a surface keeps: the theme in use and the cache of resolved styles.

The application owns the themes. `Resolve` walks the chain least specific first: the built-in light theme, the surface's theme, then the `overrides` (the nearest ancestor's override before the widget's own). For each theme the kind's default style is laid over the result, then the variant's. A style with overrides is not cached; the one without is, keyed by kind, variant, state and scale, and the cache is dropped by `SetTheme`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the surface theme, which must outlive its use here. |

### `SetTheme(const Theme*)` {#ui-themeset-settheme-const-theme-star}

`method ui::ThemeSet::SetTheme` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
bool SetTheme(const Theme* theme);
```

Uses `theme` as the surface's theme and bumps the revision; the cache is dropped. Passing the same pointer again after the theme was edited forces a re-resolve.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `theme` | the theme, or null for the built-in default alone. |
| Returns | whether any metric or font differs from the previous theme, so the caller marks layout dirty. |

### `GetTheme()` {#ui-themeset-gettheme-const}

`method ui::ThemeSet::GetTheme` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
const Theme* GetTheme() const;
```

Returns the surface's theme, or null.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | returns null when no theme was set. |
| Ownership | borrowed; the application owns it. |

### `Revision()` {#ui-themeset-revision-const}

`method ui::ThemeSet::Revision` — defined in `engine/ui/theme.h`

```cpp
u64 Revision() const;
```

Returns a counter that changes on every `SetTheme`; a widget re-resolves when it differs from the one it saw.

## `UpdatePolicy` {#ui-updatepolicy}

`enum ui::UpdatePolicy` — defined in `engine/ui/update_policy.h`

```cpp
enum class UpdatePolicy : u8 { ... }
```

How often a surface runs a frame.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="ui-updatepolicy-everyframe"></a>`EveryFrame` |  | Every call runs a frame: animations and the viewport stay smooth. |
| <a id="ui-updatepolicy-ondemand"></a>`OnDemand` |  | A frame runs the first time and then only when `Surface::NeedsFrame()` says something changed; an idle surface does no work at all. |
| <a id="ui-updatepolicy-fixedrate"></a>`FixedRate` |  | A frame runs at most `rate` times a second, whatever the caller's frame rate (a low-priority monitor). |

## `Viewport` {#ui-viewport}

`class ui::Viewport` — defined in `engine/ui/viewport.h`

```cpp
class Viewport : public Widget { ... }
```

A widget that shows a rendered world.

Per frame, after layout, `Draw` works out the size in physical pixels (`bounds * scale`, rounded), calls `ViewportBinding::OnResize` when it differs from the last size given (so a window resized ten times between two frames costs one call), then `Render`, then draws the binding's image over the bounds. A zero-size viewport (a collapsed pane) calls nothing and keeps the binding's target; a hidden or collapsed one is not drawn, so it renders nothing and changes nothing. The viewport asks for `Update` and marks itself dirty each frame, so it redraws every frame while shown. Children of the viewport (a HUD label, a gizmo handle) draw after the image inside the viewport's clip and get the pointer before the viewport does, because hit testing finds the deepest widget.

About input, it is focusable, takes the keyboard focus on a click and holds the capture while a button is down. Pointer events go to the binding in viewport pixels. Mouse look: when `SetLookButton` names a button, pressing it starts look: the surface is asked to lock the pointer (`Surface::RequestPointerLock`) and relative motion reaches the binding as `Look` deltas; where the pointer cannot be locked the viewport falls back to the captured absolute motion, as differences between positions, and logs a warning once. Releasing the button, losing the focus, being hidden or removed ends the look.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the binding, which must outlive the viewport or be cleared with `SetBinding(nullptr)`. |

## `Widget` {#ui-widget}

`class ui::Widget` — defined in `engine/ui/widget.h`

```cpp
class Widget { ... }
```

A node of the widget tree.

Children are owned (`UniquePtr`) and drawn in order, so the last child is on top and is hit-tested first. A widget's id is a hash of its parent's id and its name (its index among the siblings when the name is empty), so it is the same every time a tree is built the same way. Bounds are in surface coordinates, logical units. The state bits (hover, pressed, focused) are kept by the surface; `Disabled` follows [`ui::Widget::EffectivelyEnabled`](#ui-widget-effectivelyenabled-const).

Removal is safe from inside an event handler: while the surface dispatches, [`ui::Widget::RemoveChild`](#ui-widget-removechild-widget-star) and [`ui::Widget::RemoveFromParent`](#ui-widget-removefromparent) queue the removal and the widget dies when the dispatch ends. Destroying a widget clears the surface's references to it (focus, capture, hover).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its children; borrows the parent and the surface. |

### `CanAccept(const DragPayload&,Point)` {#ui-widget-canaccept-const-dragpayload-amp-point}

`method ui::Widget::CanAccept` — defined in `engine/ui/widget.h`

```cpp
virtual DragAction CanAccept(const DragPayload&, Point);
```

Drop target: decides whether a drag at `position` (local) would be accepted here, and what it would do. The surface asks the widget under the pointer, then its ancestors, and the first that answers other than `Nothing` is the target. The default refuses. A payload from another program has `pending` entries without data. The answer must be one of `payload.Allowed()`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | the action the drop would take (usually `payload.Requested()`), or `Nothing`. |

### `OnDragEnter(const DragPayload&,Point)` {#ui-widget-ondragenter-const-dragpayload-amp-point}

## `Button` {#ui-button}

`class ui::Button` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/buttons.h`

```cpp
class Button : public Widget { ... }
```

A push button: text and/or an icon on a themed box.

It clicks when the left button is released inside it after being pressed inside it (a press followed by a drag out and a release elsewhere does not click), and when Space or Enter goes down on it while it has the focus. `OnClick` listeners then run. The look is the `button` style of the theme in the widget's state (normal, hover, pressed, focused, disabled; a toggle's checked state too) and a variant such as `primary`; a state change repaints the widget and does not touch layout. A disabled button gets no input. The default-button flag only marks the button a dialog should press on Enter; the dialog (Day 203) acts on it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its text; borrows the icon's image. |

## `ComboBox` {#ui-combobox}

`class ui::ComboBox` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/combobox.h`

```cpp
class ComboBox : public Widget { ... }
```

A box that shows the selected item of a [`ui::ListModel`](#ui-listmodel) and opens a drop-down [`ui::ListBox`](#ui-listbox) (virtualized, so a model of 100,000 items is as cheap as one of ten).

While closed, Up and Down select the previous or next item (they run `OnSelectionChanged`), Home and End the first and last, letters select by type-ahead, and the wheel selects when `SetWheelSelects(true)`. Alt+Down, F4, Space and Enter open the list, and so does a press on the box. While open, the list has the keyboard (arrows, Home, End, PageUp, PageDown, type-ahead); Enter or a click on an item chooses it and closes the list, Escape or a click elsewhere closes it and keeps the old selection; the focus goes back to the box.

With `SetEditable(true)` the box holds a [`ui::TextField`](#ui-textfield): choosing an item puts its text in the field, and typing text equal to an item selects that item (any other text selects none), so the text and the selection never disagree. Down in the field opens the list.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the model; owns its text field and, while it is open, hands the drop-down list to the surface. |


A [`ui::WindowFrame`](#ui-windowframe) with a button row at the bottom.

`AddButton` adds a button that closes the dialog with a result. Enter (when the focus owner does not take it) presses the default button; Escape and the close button close the dialog with the cancel result (`dialog_result::kCancel` unless `SetCancelResult` says otherwise). `Close(result)` runs `OnClosed` and removes the dialog: from the surface's modal stack when it is modal (the focus goes back to its previous owner), else from its parent. Removal is deferred while an event is delivered, so a button handler may close its own dialog; do not use the dialog after `OnClosed` returns, the surface destroys it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its buttons and its content. |

## `ImageFit` {#ui-imagefit}

`enum ui::ImageFit` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/display.h`

```cpp
enum class ImageFit : u8 { ... }
```

How an [`ui::Image`](#ui-image) fills its bounds.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="ui-imagefit-stretch"></a>`Stretch` |  | The image fills the bounds, distorted if the shapes differ. |
| <a id="ui-imagefit-contain"></a>`Contain` |  | The whole image, scaled to fit, centered, keeping its shape. |
| <a id="ui-imagefit-cover"></a>`Cover` |  | The image scaled to cover the bounds, keeping its shape; the overflow is cut. |
| <a id="ui-imagefit-none"></a>`None` |  | The natural size, centered; cut by the bounds when larger. |

## `ProgressBar` {#ui-progressbar}

`class ui::ProgressBar` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/display.h`

```cpp
class ProgressBar : public Widget { ... }
```

A bar that fills from the left with a value from 0 to 1, or a segment that slides while the progress is unknown.

The colors and corner radius come from the `panel` style (the track) and the theme's accent color (the fill). The indeterminate segment moves with the surface clock, one pass per `kProgressPeriod` seconds, and the widget asks for `Update` only while it is indeterminate so that a still bar costs nothing.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns nothing. |

## `ListBox` {#ui-listbox}

`class ui::ListBox` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/list_box.h`

```cpp
class ListBox : public Widget { ... }
```

A scrolling list of text items from a model.

The list is virtual: rows are all one height, so the item under a point and the range in view come from arithmetic, and only the rows in view are laid out and drawn (a ring of cached text layouts re-used as the list scrolls); 100,000 items cost the same per frame as 100. The list scrolls with its own vertical bar and with the wheel. Click selects (with Ctrl or Shift in multiple mode), the arrow keys, PageUp, PageDown, Home and End move the current item and select it, Space selects it, Enter runs `OnActivate`; typing letters selects the next item whose text starts with what was typed (typed quickly, the letters make a prefix; the same letter repeated cycles through the items starting with it). After the model changes call `ModelChanged`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the model; owns the selection and the row cache. |

### `kWheelRows` {#ui-listbox-kwheelrows}

`variable ui::ListBox::kWheelRows` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/list_box.h`

```cpp

```

The wheel moves this many rows per notch.


## `MenuView` {#ui-menuview}

`class ui::MenuView` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/menu.h`

```cpp
class MenuView : public Widget { ... }
```

The popup that shows a [`ui::Menu`](#ui-menu): a column of rows with a highlight that follows the pointer and the arrow keys.

Up and Down move the highlight over the enabled items and wrap; Home and End jump; Enter and Space choose; a letter chooses the item with that mnemonic; Right opens a submenu (and moves into it), Left closes a submenu; hovering a submenu item opens it after `kSubmenuDelay`. Choosing a leaf item runs its action and closes every popup. A left or right arrow that the menu cannot use goes to `OnEdge` (the menu bar switches menus there). Escape and clicks outside are the surface's popup rules.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows or owns its menu (see the constructors); owns nothing else. |

### `kSubmenuDelay` {#ui-menuview-ksubmenudelay}

`variable ui::MenuView::kSubmenuDelay` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/menu.h`

## `MenuBar` {#ui-menubar}

`class ui::MenuBar` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/menu_bar.h`

```cpp
class MenuBar : public Widget { ... }
```

A row of menu titles.

A click on a title opens its menu; while one is open, moving the pointer to another title switches to it, and Left and Right in the open menu (when it has no submenu to enter or leave) do the same. The bar is also driven from the keyboard: install it with `Surface::SetMenuKeyTarget`, then F10 or a lone Alt press takes the focus and highlights the first title (the mnemonic letters are underlined), Left and Right move, Down, Enter and Space open the menu, a letter opens the title with that mnemonic, Escape (or F10 / Alt again) leaves the bar. Alt with a letter opens the matching menu from anywhere. Leaving the bar (or closing its menu) gives the focus back to the widget that had it.

| Field | Value |

## `ValueRange` {#ui-valuerange}

`struct ui::ValueRange` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
struct ValueRange { ... }
```

A range of `f64` values with an optional step.

A value snaps to the grid `min + k * step` for a whole number `k`, computed as one multiplication from the minimum, so a thousand steps drift by nothing; the grid stops at the last point not above `max`. In integer mode the step is at least 1. Without a step (0) the value is only clamped. A NaN becomes the minimum.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |
| Ownership | owns nothing. |

### `min` {#ui-valuerange-min}

`variable ui::ValueRange::min` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
f64 min;
```

The smallest value.

### `max` {#ui-valuerange-max}

`variable ui::ValueRange::max` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
f64 max;
```

The largest value; a maximum below the minimum is taken as the minimum.

### `step` {#ui-valuerange-step}

`variable ui::ValueRange::step` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
f64 step;
```

The grid spacing, 0 for none.

### `integer` {#ui-valuerange-integer}

`variable ui::ValueRange::integer` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
bool integer;
```

Whole numbers only (the step is at least 1).

### `policy` {#ui-valuerange-policy}

`variable ui::ValueRange::policy` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp
RangePolicy policy;
```

What `Apply` does with a value outside `min`..`max`.

`method ui::ValueRange::EffectiveStep` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp

```

Returns the effective step: `step`, or 1 in integer mode when that is larger.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

`method ui::ValueRange::Snap` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/range.h`

```cpp

```

Returns `value` clamped to the range and snapped to the grid.

|---|---|


## `ScrollBar` {#ui-scrollbar}

`class ui::ScrollBar` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/scroll.h`

```cpp
class ScrollBar : public Widget { ... }
```

A scroll bar along one axis, with a value in content units.

The thumb's length is the track's length times the view/content ratio, never below the theme's minimum hit size and never above the track; its place follows the value. A press on the thumb drags it (the pointer is captured, so the drag may leave the bar), a press on the track outside the thumb pages by one view towards the pointer, a press on an arrow (when shown) moves by one line. With content not larger than the view the value is 0 and the thumb fills the track. The auto-hide variant draws nothing unless the pointer is over it, it is being dragged, or it changed within the last `kFadeSeconds`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns nothing. |

### `kFadeSeconds` {#ui-scrollbar-kfadeseconds}

`variable ui::ScrollBar::kFadeSeconds` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/scroll.h`

```cpp
static constexpr f32 kFadeSeconds;
```

The seconds an auto-hide bar stays visible after its value changed.

`variable ui::ScrollBar::kLineFraction` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/scroll.h`

```cpp

```

The distance a press on an arrow moves, as a fraction of the view when no line step was set.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |

## `ScrollView` {#ui-scrollview}

`class ui::ScrollView` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/scroll.h`

```cpp
class ScrollView : public Widget { ... }
```

One child that may be larger than the view, with scroll bars.

The content is the first child; the bars follow. The content gets its preferred size along an axis that scrolls (at least the view's size, so it fills a small view) and exactly the view's size along one that does not. The view clips its content's drawing and hit testing to its own bounds (the bars sit on top). The wheel scrolls by `LineStep` per notch and is handled only when it moved something, so a wheel at the end of the content bubbles on to an outer view; a focus change inside the content scrolls the focused widget into view. There is no inertia or elastic overscroll.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the content and the bars. |

### `ScrollView(StringView)` {#ui-scrollview-scrollview-stringview}

## `Splitter` {#ui-splitter}

`class ui::Splitter` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/splitter.h`

```cpp
class Splitter : public Widget { ... }
```

Two or more panes along one axis, separated by draggable handles.

Every pane has a relative size (a fraction of the room left after the handles); the sizes follow the splitter when it resizes, in proportion, and a pane never gets less than its minimum (`SetMinSize`) while the room allows it. A press on a handle captures the pointer and drags the boundary between the two panes beside it (the resize cursor shows over a handle); a double click on a handle restores the sizes the panes were added with. A pane marked collapsible (`SetCollapsible`) collapses when a drag takes it below half its minimum and opens again when a drag takes it back over that, and `SetPaneCollapsed` does the same from code. The layout is plain numbers: `Fractions` and `SetFractions` (and the collapsed flags) are what a layout file would store.

`Horizontal` places the panes left to right (the handles are vertical bars); `Vertical` top to bottom. The setters run no listener; a drag and a double click run `OnChanged`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its panes. |

## `TabView` {#ui-tabview}

`class ui::TabView` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/tab_view.h`

```cpp
class TabView : public Widget { ... }
```

Tab headers over pages.

Each tab has a title, an optional image and an optional close button, and owns one page (a widget). Only the selected page is shown (the others are collapsed, not destroyed, so their state is kept); a lazy tab (`AddLazyTab`) builds its page, once, the first time it is selected. A click on a header selects its tab. With the view focused, Left, Right, Home and End move the selection; Ctrl+Tab and Ctrl+PageDown select the next tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab and Ctrl+PageUp the previous one (wrapping), also when the focus is inside a page. When the headers do not fit, two arrows appear at the right end and the wheel over the headers scrolls them; selecting a tab scrolls it into view. A close button asks `OnCloseRequested`; the program decides and calls `CloseTab`.

`Select` and the other setters run no listener; the user's actions (mouse and keys) run `OnSelectionChanged`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its pages (as children) and its header layouts. |

## `TextValidator` {#ui-textvalidator}

`alias ui::TextValidator` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/text_field.h`

```cpp
using TextValidator = bool (*)(void* user, const TextField* field, StringView proposed);
```

Decides whether a proposed text may replace the field's text: the function gets the pointer given to `SetValidator`, the field and the whole text the edit would make (valid for the call only), and returns false to keep the old text.

## `TextField` {#ui-textfield}

`class ui::TextField` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/text_field.h`

```cpp
class TextField : public Widget { ... }
```

An editable line of text.

Offsets are byte offsets into the UTF-8 text and are always on grapheme boundaries (`text::NextBoundary` and `text::PreviousBoundary` over the field's layout): a combining mark, a flag, a ZWJ sequence or a Hangul syllable moves and deletes as one unit. Only `Text` events insert, so a key the field does not know does nothing; there is no input method and no complex-script shaping (Phase 14 scope). Ctrl+Left and Ctrl+Right move by word ([`ui::WordStart`](#ui-wordstart-stringview-u32), [`ui::WordEnd`](#ui-wordend-stringview-u32)). The field scrolls sideways to keep the caret in view.

The keys are Left, Right, Home, End (Shift extends the selection, Ctrl moves by word), Backspace, Delete (Ctrl: by word), Ctrl+A, Ctrl+C / Ctrl+Insert, Ctrl+X / Shift+Delete, Ctrl+V / Shift+Insert, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y, Enter (`OnSubmit`), Escape (`OnCancel`). Tab, Up and Down are left to the surface and the parents. The mouse: a click places the caret, Shift+click extends, a drag selects (the pointer is captured), a double click selects a word, a triple click all.

Edits go through one path that applies, in order, the read-only flag, the maximum length (in clusters; a longer insertion is cut), and the validator; an edit that changes nothing is not recorded. Consecutive typing, and consecutive Backspace or Delete presses, are one undo step. A password field draws bullets, refuses copy and cut, moves and edits by cluster, and treats its whole text as one word (word movement, word deletion and a double click go to the ends), so the spaces do not show.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its text, its undo history and its layouts; borrows the validator's user pointer and the surface's clipboard. |

### `kWidthInIcons` {#ui-textfield-kwidthinicons}


The preferred width, as a multiple of the theme's icon size.

## `Slider` {#ui-slider}

`class ui::Slider` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/value_widgets.h`

```cpp
class Slider : public Widget { ... }
```

A thumb on a track that chooses a value in a range.

Horizontal (minimum at the left) or vertical (minimum at the bottom). The value snaps to the step exactly ([`ui::ValueRange`](#ui-valuerange)). The pointer drags the thumb (captured, so the drag may leave the slider); a press on the track jumps there and goes on dragging, or, with `SetTrackPages(true)`, moves one page towards the pointer. Keys: arrows by a step, PageUp/PageDown by a page, Home and End to the ends; the wheel only when switched on. `OnChanged` runs whenever the user changes the value (while dragging too), `OnCommit` when a drag ends or a key changed it. `SetValue` changes the value without running either.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns nothing. |

### `kPageFraction` {#ui-slider-kpagefraction}

`variable ui::Slider::kPageFraction` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/value_widgets.h`

```cpp
static constexpr f64 kPageFraction;
```

The page as a fraction of the range when none was set.

### `kKeyFraction` {#ui-slider-kkeyfraction}

`variable ui::Slider::kKeyFraction` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/value_widgets.h`

```cpp

```

The arrow key's distance as a fraction of the range when the range has no step.

`variable ui::Slider::kTrackBorders` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/value_widgets.h`

```cpp

```

The thickness of the track as a multiple of the theme's border width.


The length of a tick mark as a fraction of the thumb's thickness.


The preferred length of the track as a multiple of the thumb's thickness.

## `SpinBox` {#ui-spinbox}

`class ui::SpinBox` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/value_widgets.h`

```cpp
class SpinBox : public Widget { ... }
```

A number with up and down buttons.

The box is drawn in the `textfield` style; the left part holds an embedded [`ui::TextField`](#ui-textfield) and the right part the buttons (up above, down below). A press on a button, an arrow key or a wheel notch changes the value by the step, PageUp and PageDown by ten steps; every change goes through the range, so the value is always in range and on the grid. The field takes digits, a sign (when the range has negative values) and a decimal point (when `SetDecimals` is above 0) and nothing else; Enter, and losing the focus, commit the text (a text that is not a number, or that the range rejects, goes back to the value), and Escape reverts it. Home and End move the caret in the field.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its text field. |

The width of the button column as a multiple of the theme's icon size.

## `WindowFrame` {#ui-windowframe}

`class ui::WindowFrame` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/window_frame.h`

```cpp
class WindowFrame : public Widget { ... }
```

A framed panel: title bar (title text and an optional close button), content, and an optional footer row, in the `window` and `titlebar` styles.

Dragging the title bar moves the frame; it stays inside its parent's bounds (the surface or a layer). With `SetResizable(true)` the edges and corners resize it (the resize cursors show over them), down to a minimum and up to the parent's bounds. The frame is positioned by its bounds, so put it in a parent that leaves the bounds of its children alone (the root, the popup or modal layer, a plain widget), not in a layout container. The close button, when shown, calls `CloseRequested`, which runs `OnCloseRequested`; the frame does not remove itself.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its content and footer. |

### `kEdge` {#ui-windowframe-kedge}

`variable ui::WindowFrame::kEdge` — defined in `engine/ui/widgets/window_frame.h`

```cpp
static constexpr f32 kEdge;
```

The width of the resize band along each edge, in logical units.




The smallest width a resize leaves, as a multiple of the theme's icon size, unless `SetMinSize` says otherwise.
